# Robert Pattinson Seedance Mini Production Package — v2

Robert Pattinson Seedance Mini Production Package

Robert Pattinson Seedance clip packets and reference tracker — v2 (2026-09-17: fidelity fixes, see “What v2 changed”)

Full dramatic adaptation of chapter one the accident.docx followed by ROBERT PATTINSON.docx, ending at “Thank you.” after the dislike wish. No asylum/hospital screenplay or later supermarket episodes have been imported. The accident chapter mentions a hospital destination, but does not show arrival.

Each packet is an independent, maximum 15-second generation brief. Historical events are delivered as the woman’s dialogue at the restaurant in the base cut; they are not automatically reenacted. The valentine recollection is the one separately staged memory already built into the base edit. Internal prose informs performance; selected opening car prose is retained as the woman’s voiceover, and seven lines of Robert’s chapter-A prose are proposed as his (marked VO PROPOSED). The complete extracted source text is preserved in the final appendix for comparison, including unfinished phrases.

Package: 162 clips, 57 reference records, approximately 30 minutes 45 seconds of planned footage before trimming. No clip exceeds 15 seconds. Every clip has a reference-matrix row, a complete prompt and a production-tracker row. The long confession is preserved word for word; it is split at sentence boundaries only (v2), never mid-clause.

## Before generating

The writing and reference breakdown are complete, but source conflicts, missing transition decisions, unavailable reference pixels, and the exact Seedance Mini service remain approval gates. A packet tagged HOLD is not safe to send unchanged. Existing image files below are located candidates, not visually approved: image reads supplied no pixels and downloading failed. Do not spend generation credits until the required images and decisions for that packet are locked.

Use only the fenced prompt for the selected clip, with that clip’s actual approved image attachments. Reference IDs are project identifiers, not Seedance syntax. Bind each attached file to the selected interface’s image slots; never paste bare IDs as if the model can retrieve files. If slots are limited, create a single approved opening-frame composition containing the relevant people, costumes, set and props; do not silently omit a identity or key object. Match prior approved closing frame where continuing an action, but do not replay the preceding clip. Never attach the full script as an image reference.

“Mini” does not uniquely identify the provider/interface. Its image limit, duration control, audio behavior and celebrity-reference policy need confirmation. ByteDance’s official Seedance 2.0 launch describes 15-second output and multimodal references, but those specifications are not assumed to apply to an unidentified Mini endpoint. It also describes authorization requirements for real-person reference images. Official product source. No videos have been submitted and no likeness or voice acceptance is promised. Do not bypass provider restrictions; use an authorized depiction or separately approved fictional casting if required. Voice directions describe performance, not cloned celebrity audio.

Provisional visual format: photorealistic, portrait 9:16, restrained naturalistic color, normal skin texture, no added captions or music. These are production choices, not words from the manuscripts. If the film master is landscape, change the ratio everywhere before creating the first master reference. Clip timings are editorial estimates, not guaranteed generation performance. Record or rehearse the exact dialogue; if it overruns, split further instead of rushing or rewriting it. Base speech packets carry up to about 40 words per 15 seconds (roughly 160 words a minute, the pace the source directs for her: “speaking more quickly”, “in a rush”), and a clip is cut only at the end of a sentence.

## Source order and decisions

Decision

Evidence

Working treatment

Document order

A follows cafe observation through injured passenger’s ride; B opens with later kidnapping drive

A first, B second. B’s chapter labels 5 and 7 show missing material, not permission to invent it.

Woman’s name

A p 6 says Paige; B calls her Stella and uses Stella as a POV heading

Label her WOMAN in directions; keep spoken Paige verbatim. Treat as one provisional character, name unresolved. No global rename to Sophie.

Victim’s fate

A ends en route with man in car; B omits him

HOLD car fragment bridge until author decides whether/when he reaches hospital. Do not erase him from rear seat and imply a drop-off.

Restaurant bridge

B jumps from coastal driving to used dishes and tea

Hard editorial scene cut, no invented meal or arrival. Need author-approved missing transition for finished story.

Accident intentionality

B opening says an accident would have to be; later woman denies wishing for accident

Preserve ambiguity. Do not depict a magical or engineered crash.

Weather

A explicitly very hot, sun beating down; B rain begins outside restaurant

Sunny dry accident, wet restaurant departure and worsening storm afterward. Earlier rainy accident images are conflicting variants.

Robert cafe wardrobe

A brown cap, concealing glasses, hugely baggy sweatpants; B later long black shiny coat

Lock exact cafe outfit independently of candidate image jeans; later coat continuity requires approval. Sunglasses left on cafe table in A. Cap loss not supplied.

Woman wardrobe

A frumpy frock; B frumpy coat and glasses; current image candidate sweater/trousers

Source frock+later coat is base. Candidate identity may be reused, candidate outfit not silently substituted.

Barista pronouns

A changes she to he; name Robert Patterson

One separate male-presenting barista provisionally; confirm. Do not confuse with Pattinson.

Her car make

A old station wagon; B another Volvo is nearly hit

Existing Volvo wagon is a visual choice, not proof source specifies her make. Keep near-miss car separate.

Ocean side

B explicitly “left (or was it our right?)”

Keep ocean out of tight shots until direction locked.

Cupcakes

Historical purchase 12 minis; present purchase 24 white-iced yellow cakes with pink/purple sprinkles; recent reference five blue cakes in six slots

Three distinct versions. Base packets preserve source 24-pack. Approval can replace all CURRENT pack references with blue six-pack; historical 12-pack stays separate. Full six needed before removal.

Store

Source Ralphs; earlier visual request mentions Vons packaging

Base Ralphs. Branded pack/store mismatch must be approved, not concealed.

Aisle geometry

Source hair grazes apple juice; latest user direction lengthwise, backs to empty aisle, directly opposite

Explicit adaptation: latest blocking, near enough a side shelf to take a juice carton. No backs against shelves.

Prove it duplicate

B p 12 repeats line at Robert-to-Stella POV handoff

First repeated event filmed ONCE; later p 13 Prove it is a separate utterance.

Childhood timeline

Second grade, seven, nine, seventh grade, later seven-year abstinence do not align neatly

Preserve spoken chronology; do not silently repair ages or create a timeline graphic.

Fruit hair ties

B says raspberries then apples

Preserve words in base dialogue; design only after author chooses intended fruit.

Source fragments

“I thought”, unfinished road sentence, ambiguous wish/dispatcher attribution, punctuation notes

No invented completions. Spoken doubtful lines identified; complete original remains in appendix.

First flame and last relight

B jumps from cupcake placement to burning flame, later lights candle again

First-light insert marked proposed. Last relight requires an explicit chosen earlier extinction; HOLD reset rather than impossible continuous flame.

Hand occupancy

Both hands clasped immediately before she lights candle

Proposed brief release/rejoin included; also set straw down before taking both hands. No extra hands.

Payment

Waitress takes card; following source says check paid, no return shown

Treat as editorial omission; do not duplicate card in both hands. Card retrieval may be added only with approval.

## What v2 changed (2026-09-17)

Each row is a fidelity correction against the two source chapters. The clips named are v2 ids.

D007

Wish reassigned to Robert (he plucked the lash; “Ha!” and the snort are hers; “I was satisfied that it really wouldn’t prove anything” is her relief about HIS wish). Author may reverse.

A012

“Yes / Is he in danger of being hit?” share one quotation in the source → one speaker, the dispatcher. Author may hand “Yes” back to Robert.

C090

Source restored: she rips off two fingernails and it is “really quite disgusting”; the disdain on “girls” is directed.

V001–V005

“Red heart” removed; the source says an awful doily thing made of magazine pictures. Design the valentine from those words only.

C036

“something in mean to me” read as a typo → “something mean to me”. Author confirms.

B003/B008

The coast is back: one wide of the wagon on the coastal road with the waves alive. The side is a production pick (note it on the master), never a tight-shot dependency.

C001

Restaurant named: the Reel Inn (source p 17), which also fits the coastal drive.

A001/A002/A003/A013/A014/A022/A023

Robert VO added from his own chapter-A prose, verbatim, mirroring the woman’s VO in B001–B003. Each is one line she can drop.

C004–C094 (now fewer)

The confession re-split at sentence boundaries at about 40 words per 15 seconds (~160 wpm, her “speaking more quickly … in a rush”), never mid-clause. Same words, fewer clips.

## Ambiguities still open for the author

Which lines carry the mocking eyelash wish (v2: Robert) · whether “Yes” on the ambulance call is Robert or the dispatcher (v2: dispatcher) · who says “Hey - you!” (v2: Robert, softening at once) · “It was like I was bullying her, but I wasn’t” sits outside the quotation marks (v2: hers) · “The girl rolled her eyes” — the woman as a child or Robert watching her now (v2: her now) · raspberries then apples · “something in mean” (v2: “something mean”) · Paige in A, Stella in B · what happens to the injured man · how they reach the Reel Inn · where the cap and sweatpants go before the long black coat · the first candle’s extinction before she lights it again.

## How to use each packet

Approve required identities, wardrobe and opening state; resolve every HOLD affecting the clip.

Attach only its reference set, labeled by role. Prefer one approved scene-opening frame plus identity/prop detail images over unrelated collages.

Paste the fenced prompt. Background is psychological context only. CURRENT CLIP is the sole list of visible events.

Generate and inspect identities, left/right positions, exact speech, object counts, hands, weather and ending state.

Save the approved clip and its last usable frame. Record output filename, take, approval, and any continuity issue in the tracker. Do not advance using an unapproved closing state.

The cinematography is a practical adaptation. Prose descriptions of inner thoughts are acted, not all recited. Dialogue words are preserved where legible; paragraph line breaks, quotation boundaries and attribution are normalized for performance. Suspicious draft fragments are flagged instead of rewritten. Optional visual flashbacks below replace the corresponding restaurant picture while keeping its exact dialogue as adult woman voiceover; they are not extra time or extra dialogue in the base edit.

Produce these in dependency order. Do not regenerate a good identity merely to fix a prop. Before commissioning a new image, check whether an existing approved shot can supply it.

Lock woman name/wardrobe, Robert cafe outfit and later coat. Make neutral front/three-quarter/profile portraits, full-body outfit views, then wet variants. Keep faces identical across variants. R01–R07.

Cast barista, injured phone man, waitress, guard and separate valentine fan. For pedestrian include standing, prone, supported, and rear-seat states without changing face/clothes. R08–R12.

Approve exact text-free cafe interior and daylight. Build matching exterior master and reverse across-street view showing window, counter, door-adjacent table, parked hiding car, crossing, victim point and rescue curb. R13–R14.

Inspect existing heart collage and white fridge shot. Derive handover, fixed magnet position, fridge-wide and bin shot; need consistent scissors. Approve distinct memory meeting outfits/place and Robert home outfit. R15/R28/R56–R57.

Inspect Volvo sheet. Derive single-view exterior front/rear/sides; left driver/right passenger cabin; rear seat/loading door; exact passenger-looking-down insert; controls and worn seat close-ups. No cupcakes in early cabin. R16–R19/R31–R32.

Make victim phone intact/shattered and Robert separate phone; cafe one-latte/script/sunglasses table close-up. R29–R30.

Lock coastal travel direction/other Volvo. Build restaurant master and both eyeline reverses, exact tea/plates/check/card, hand and tiny eyelash details. R20–R21/R33–R35.

Create same wagon and characters soaked at restaurant parking lot; matching windshield heavy-rain interior with heater hand position; store lot and fixed parking space. R07/R22–R24.

Create supermarket layout master and clean plates: bakery, checkout, home goods, entrance/guard and wide snack/juice aisle; seated pair exactly lengthwise/opposite. R25–R27.

Choose 24-white source pack OR six-blue visual pack. Produce sealed full, opened full, opened minus one, single matching cupcake, candle unlit/lit/smoking. Historical 12-pack remains separate if flashback used. R36–R38/R55.

Make matchbox open/closed, spent match, exact cash/receipt/bag sequence; one apple juice box with straw attached and detached; sensor/palm-frond doorway before/after with hail. R27/R38–R40.

Only if visual backstory approved: cast child woman/Samantha/teacher/mother/friend/parents/Gemma; classroom and playground masters; hair ties/notebook/thermos, memorial strawberries, pony bedroom, wish props, snow, reunion and cinema. R43–R55. These are NOT required to shoot base confession.

## Generation packets

The sequence below is the edit order. V recollection clips are placed after the first watcher clip and before return to present-day cafe. All prompts state present opening/ending conditions; no prior clip is assumed to be remembered by the model.

### A001 Woman across the street

Source: A p 1. Target: 8 seconds. Gate: VO PROPOSED — APPROVE OR DROP.

```
CLIP A001 — 8 SECONDS MAXIMUM
Photorealistic grounded fiction, portrait 9:16, natural skin texture and restrained color. No added subtitles, titles, logos or music. Keep approved reference identities and geometry exact.
ATTACH THESE REFERENCES
R01: approved robert identity reference.
R02: approved woman identity reference.
R03: approved robert cafe outfit reference.
R04: approved woman accident outfit reference.
R13: approved cafe interior master reference.
R14: approved cafe street geography reference.
Use the actual attached images assigned to these roles. Reference IDs do not retrieve images. Identity and prop references do not override the opening frame composition or lighting.
TONE/PERFORMANCE
Hot sunny ordinary day; grounded awkward dark comedy. Robert is guarded about recognition but acts to help. The woman is fixated and nervous, not a composed seductress. No supernatural effects. Source A chapter one the accident.docx
```

Library identity: libfile_2bb3f42790d48191ac75642eef217ce4.

Page 1

There was that woman again. Short, kind of - stubby looking - and wearing a frock that could only be described as frumpy. I could see her beady eyes, peeking out at me {positioned directly} behind a parked car. (I would have to do something about her - maybe call the police or something). They were never any harm - they just wanted an autograph usually. And when they said my name - Robert Pattinson - it looked like their insides were going to combust. Once I had seen one with a pair of scissors. But it turned out she was just cutting up some pictures of a magazine to make me a valentine. That was fine. I had taken the awful doilie thing from her sweaty ugly hands and positioned a fake smile on my narrow lips. “Thank you.” I had said, beaming at her nastily, until she finally went away. But not before babbling about something for a little while - maybe that I was different in person than in interviews she’d seen - or just the same. It was one or the other, and I could never remember which. I hadn’t thrown the valentine in the trash immediately. I tacked it up on my fridge for a couple of days. Had a laugh whenever I walked by, thinking of how her scissors had been threatening at first. But I got sick of it, and eventually, it did get chucked in the bin. Was this the same woman? To be perfectly honest - all (my many followers/fans) {these women} had blended into each other at some point. Oh sure - some were old, some were young. Some were frumpy, and some were actually quite pleasant looking. But when you looked into their eyes - pathetic (empty?) eyes, there was more desperation than I could handle for more than a few minutes. So I would make pleasant conversation with them, make sure that they didn’t want to kill me. But this cafe - the barista there - she knew who I was and had decided it didn’t matter.

Page 2

Just didn’t tolerate the nonsense. Perhaps out of jealousy - I didn’t know, and I didn’t really care - he would send anyone away who even gave me a second look. I felt bad for depriving him of so many customers, so whenever I ordered a latte, I paid for about seven. Sometimes he made them, and sometimes he didn’t. If he did, he’d just have to throw them away. And believe me - I can’t drink seven lattes all by myself - believe me - I’ve tried. Even so, despite the (?) benevolence of the barista, whose name happened to be Robert Patterson, I still entered the cafe wearing an ugly brown baseball cap, and glasses that hid most of my face. I wasn’t in shape since the last movie I’d done 4 months ago. And I was wearing these ugly baggy sweatpants that were about three sizes too big for me. I doubted anyone would recognize me today. And if they did, they’d probably just run away in horror. * * * I sipped my latte - just the one today, and before settling into the script I had brought, I took a look at the passersby outside. No one particularly interesting. There seemed to be a new fashion trend of hiked up sweatpants that I didn’t much care for. About six people walked by with their waistbands turned inside out, and not all of them were women. The man wearing his waistband overturned was chatting on the phone as he crossed the street - gesticulating with his arm as he gabbed, not looking where he was going. The latte had steamed up my sunglasses. I turned to see if Robert had a napkin for me, and from outside, there came an almighty screech. I whipped around. I saw that the waistband man - the gabby phone man, was now lying on the floor in the middle of the street. His glasses had been knocked askew, and there was a car, two feet to his left.

Page 3

My table was right next to the door. Without really thinking about what I was doing, I wrenched it open, and appeared by his side. The man’s arm twitched towards his phone, which was laying broken and shattered a few feet from him. Apparently, he was not dead. Then he saw me, sort of hovering over his not-dead body, pretty unsure as to what to do. “Robert Pattinson?” he gasped. I realized now that I had left my glasses on the table “Am I - in heaven?” he gasped. There was that lady - the one that I always saw. She had caught the accident too, she was on the other side of the street, staring open mouthed, transfixed and not moving. Apparently she didn’t know what to do either. But as the light changed, she stepped forward into the street, as if in a trance. There weren’t many other people around. The driver that had hit him had left immediately, tires screeching. I wondered if the girl had seen the l “Hey!” I said “Did you see the plate of the guy who hit him?” She goggled at me, but then she said “Oh yeah - kind of - it was - J. 7. 9. Or…9…7…J…something like that Ok, so no. It didn’t matter. “We have to get him to a hospital.” I said. She wasn’t goggling anymore, but (she did look kind of stupid) - was it my imagination that she still looked kind of stupid. It seemed obvious we needed to take immediate action, but she was just standing there. She wasn’t even looking at him - she was looking at me. “Oh, alright.” She said. “I can take him.” “What? No. Just call an ambulance” I said, and I reached into my pocket to dial the number. “Yes Is he in danger of being hit?” Can you get him out of the street?

Page 4

I hadn’t realized I’d be implicated beyond the phone call. Of course they couldn’t get here as fast as all that. And what if he was hit again - by another car, this time, not out of negligence, but because of the situation - how could anyone see him laying down on the cement as he was. A vision of him - blood splaying everywhere. And some mom in her suv, her kids wailing in the back seat, and probably throwing graham crackers. Sighing, I hoisted his upper body into my arms. I wasn’t too pleased to have all his sweat on me - for it was a very hot day, and the sun had been beating down on him ever since he lay in the street. A few cars stopped to ogle at the situation, but if anyone recognized me, it would take all the longer. I waved them away, mouthing “it’s handled, it’s being handled.” In truth, that was a bit of an exageration. Oh, sure I went to the gym every morning. Or at least it looked like I did. But I often spent the better part of my time there, sipping on a latte (my second of the day), and reading a book. In any case, he sighed happily as I man-handled him - perhaps getting more handsy than would have been necessary, had I the upper body strength to hoist him properly. In any case, when I finally got him safely onto the sidewalk, my own and his sweat now combining…I let him collapse in a heap at my feet. His eyes were closed now, but I could feel his pulse beneath my fingers. And where had that woman gone? She had been no help in the whole carrying situation. She wasn’t even here anymore. At least that answered the stalker question. If shirking responsibility was more important, than… Just then, she appeared, at the wheel of an old station wagon. Her eyes were vaguely panic stricken, and I was afraid she was going to hit us - we were not too far from the street. Did she even have control

Page 5

of the wheel. She pulled up alongside us, and got out of the car, not bothering to close her door. Then she made a beeline to the man, who was half in my arms, half on the sidewalk, and began patiently dragging him towards her car. “Wait! What are you doing? The ambulance is on its way.” I said, barely able to think about what I was saying. “Are they?” she said, looking around. Well, no. I supposed they were not here yet. But they would be here. I could hear sirens vaguely in the distance. And I didn’t want them to find me standing here alone - apparently a liar. “We have to get him to a hospital,” she stated urgently. It turned out she wasn’t quite as weak as she looked - as her 5’4” frame and 160 pounds might have implied. She got him into the car all by herself Was she - kidnapping him? It didn’t feel safe for her to take this man by herself. He was practically unconscious. What if she was not some kind-hearted citizen after all. What if - I couldn’t think, particularly what she might do with him. Rape him, perhaps? That seemed unlikely. Steal from him? She could easily have taken his wallet while he was there on the street. Even so, I didn’t like the idea of her just taking him. Off into “Are you coming?” she asked me, waiting expectantly on the driver’s side. I looked off into the direction of the sirens. I didn’t think they’d get here before she’d driven off. And for some reason, it sounded like they were farther away now. Weren’t we in a large city? Maybe there was a lot of traffic? Well, the main thing is, she didn’t seem to know who I was. I could just get out at the hospital and be done with it. (I sighed) and got in on the passenger side.

Page 6

Her car was really quite beat up. The leather was crackly in several places on my seat and there were several mustard-colored stains. “The seat belt works, you just have to yank it a couple times,” she said. “Ok, thanks.” I said, already having giving up. This wouldn’t be a long ride. I didn’t want to get too comfy. I thought she might’ve tried to make conversation while we rode, afterall, this had been her idea - taking him in the car with us. But she didn’t say anything as she drove. Her fingers, with bits of black nail polish, drummed impatiently on the steering wheel at red lights. With not much else to do, I took a look at my shabby surroundings. Her car was not filthy exactly, but it was far from clean. Beyond the stains of mustard which were defensible - stains were notoriously hard to (fix), there were crumpled up bits of paper everywhere. Receipts, probably. And there were empty take out containers, a squashed cup, some napkins that had grown dirty just from lying on the floor and being kicked around. “What’s this,” I said, picking up one of the crumpled papers. It was not a reciept after all. “Edward kissed bella” I read at the top of the page. Oh no. Oh no. This couldn’t be happening. This was a bad dream. It was an exerept from the twilight book. The page was rough around the edges - chewed? Had someone gnawed the page out of its place in the book? Surely that wasn’t right. All around the words were confusing markings - half of the page was underlined, and there were arrows pointing everywhere Caution, surely, was the best way to go about this. There were lots of people who read the books who didn’t know who I was. Perhaps she had - prosopagnosia (face-blindness). “Hey - you!” “Um - sorry, I didn’t quite catch your name,” I said, trying to sound kind. “Paige,” she said, without looking away from the road.

Page 7

Odd, really - her complacency, it now struck me. And why hadn’t she asked my name back. Should I lie? She looked - quite nervous, now that I looked at her properly. Her eyes seemed stuck on the “wide” setting …in that eyes-open-wide like a guppy, and her fingers were white on the wheel. Perhaps the druming had not been impatient but frantic. “Err…how far is the hospital?” I asked, conversationally. “Oh, it’s not far,” she said, now glancing at me for a split second. When our eyes met, hers, if it was at all possible, opened even wider. They were going to pop out of her head I suddenly worried.

Source B ROBERT PATTINSON.docx

Library identity: libfile_fc126ae73e90819188e271ed21b04e39.

Page 1

They did not understand Robert Pattinson, like I did. They could not. An accident, it would have to be. And I, his savior. Savior, or captor. What difference did it make, really? We would both be entrenched, sharing the same puddle, stuck together like glue, for a time. It was the same thing. “Are you…kidnapping me?” he asked, his eyes crinkling with humor. He wasn’t mad, just incredulous. When he got no reply, when I did not turn, when I instead, cranked the radio to a volume that was almost intolerable, he tried to jump out of the car. No use, the doors were locked. And the windows. I had made sure of that. He reached across me, accidentally manhandling my chest in his haste. I felt a thrill. The car swerved to the left, out of the lane, almost hitting a volvo and I steadied it with one hand, the other trying to pull his hand off me. “Are you crazy?” I asked him, chiding him like a child, shaking my head, then staring back at the road. He had managed to hit the button unlocking the door but I clicked it back into place a moment later as he had been too stunned by the near collision to even open the door. “You can’t kidnap me” he said, with a bitter taste in his mouth. I’m…bigger than you. I’m older than you, probably. He sighed, settling back into his seat for the time being. Staring out the window, now, with mild interest, {at the horizon drawing closer in front of us.} Outside, the great lapping waves were to our left (or was it our right?). We were flying by - the waves very much alive.

Page 2

“I didn’t want to tell you. I wanted you to fall in love with me. For me.” She looked up at me. Was she actually crying? Dear god. Would the torments never cease? This day, already, had been so long. I brushed the fringe hurriedly out of my eyes, in the same motion, waving this away, this insane notion What? I said. “What?!” I asked again, now screaming, now getting a little angry. She jumped at my sudden outburst, spilling a tiny bit of tea, and righting the spoon in the dish. Why did they even have tea here? This was a fish place, after all. “Ok.” She said. “Ok. I’ll tell you.” And she took a deep breath Chapter 5 Her hands were clasped together, knuckes white, over the table. She was smoothing her short stubby fingers, one over the other, as though comforting herself. She probably did need to calm herself down, I thought savagely. But I did not feel bad for her. She had gotten herself into this mess. For a second, I hated her. Hated the long dark bangs that fell into her eyes. Hated the ugly glasses she was wearing, the way her frumpy coat fell on her broad shoulders, making them look even bigger. I didn’t stop this malice from creeping

Page 3

into my eyes as I looked at her. She had to take responsibility for her actions. I was never one for chivalry. If women wanted to be equal, then they could not pretend to be these sensitive does all the time. Wide eyes or not, she would pay the same penalty had she been a large man, I would take her to task to the same degree as if she’d been a large man. “It started when I was in second grade. I got really angry at this kid. She had this infuriatingly neat handwriting, you see. And she didn’t write her words out like normal either. She always left - extra space. Like, one word would take up a whole line, the way she drew her letters out. Each one was long and flat. And the worst part was - I think she thought it made her prettier or something. I think she liked herself better because of it.” This was a strange way to start the story indeed, and I glared at her, willing her to hurry. However, I didn’t want to interrupt lest she lose her nerve. To say whatever she was going to say. “And then - things started spilling. I remember my teacher, Miss Ulhavey, well, she had really bad breath, and always had this large thermos that she carried around with her. Even when she was helping us. It leaked is the thing. I don’t think she knew that until one day, she sat with that girl - her name was Samantha - and the thermos leaked all over her paper - all over her perfectly formed letters. I don’t think either of them noticed till her paper, and then her whole notebook, was sopping wet. That wasn’t the only thing. She had these two pigtail holders - they

Page 4

both had little strawberries on them. The first day she got them, she brought them in and was showing everyone. Strawberries. The girl rolled her eyes. Who cares about strawberries? So on the day that her notebook got all messed up, I found them. I found them on the playground. There was this little place that no one really went, under the slide. And one day I walked in there cause I didn’t have anyone, well (she looked away). And then looked back, speaking more quickly. All-my-friends-were-gone-that-day. Anyway. Her strawberry hair ties, her stupid strawberry hair ties were there. I picked them up of course, thinking I’d give them back to her. But when she saw them in my hand - she told the teacher I’d stolen them. Then lots of other things started happening to her. She had a pet fish named Evelyn, and it died. And the hair clips, well, they got recalled. Apparently they were made out of lead. And her mom had to pull her out during school one day and take them back from her.” It was like I was bullying her, but I wasn’t. This was getting to be too much. I didn’t care about her stupid friend’s stupid fish dying. Who named their fish Evelyn anyway? “Can you please.” I said. “Get to the point.” Shock crossed her face. (She looked stunned.) “Sorry.” She said. When I was in, like seventh grade, I started putting candles in cupcakes. You know how you can make a wish, one day a year, on your birthday? And that wish will definitely come true? I eyed her skeptically. It’s like - if I just wish for something hard enough, it will always come true.

Page 5

When I was about seven, I wished for a pony. My parents wheeled it in the next day - a little brown Pony named Shiloh. She was really hard to take care of. I had to brush her hair everyday, and it got so knotted. She looked up, begging me to understand how knotted a pony’s hair can get. I had absolutely no experience in this avenue but I arranged my face into what I thought was a sympathetic expression. At least she was getting somewhere. As you can imagine, it’s not easy keeping a pony in your room. My parents didn’t have enough money leftover for a stable - they’d spent all of their christmas budget on the pony. And it was only July.” She paused, then, to look up at me with sudden inspiration - dark brown eyes through tiny stubby lashes. “My birthday’s in July.” It seemed like she wanted me to say something. Why was she telling me this? Oh for heaven’s sake. She thought it made her special. And I supposed if I’d cared about her, maybe I would be interested in this fact. But something about her story, though she had evidently never taken a journalism class to know what details to include and which to omit, was compelling, and, in spite of myself, I told her “Oh wow! July. That’s a nice time for a birthday.” Was it? I supposed it was. It was in the summer and all. She could have parties outside, no thunderstorms. (And no school to depress you.) “Thank you.” She said, satisfied, and looked down again, continuing with her story. “I was more careful after that. The next year I didn’t wish for anything. You see, I hadn’t known that wishes would come true. I thought

Page 6

Still, things kept happening to me, when I wanted them to. Well, the thing is - I didn’t really want them to. I would have some errant thought, and then it would come true. Not immediately, but maybe, like, the next day, or - a week from then. Once, I was thinking about how nice it would be to have a new dress, and one of my eyelashes happened to fall on a piece of paper I was holding, and a little gust of wind blew it away. Then the new dress came in the mail. I don’t even know who sent it to me! Anyway, when I was about 9, Samantha… Samantha said something in mean to me in class one day and then…Stella looked up from under her lashes, and I could see she was weighing the odds of saying whatever it was that she was going to say: “She died.” Against my will, my mouth opened like a guppy. “But you didn’t mean to,” I said, finding myself assuring her. The villain. But was she really the villain? She was just a kid who had made a wish. I found myself remembering countless times I had wished someone would die without really wanting them to. “Yes!” she said, thankful for my words, seizing them. “I didn’t mean to make it happen. You know I didn’t. I had been playing in the playground after she said the mean thing - it was something about my pig tail holders, how they were only raspberries - not as good as her strawberry ones. And I knew it was true. Apples are not as good as strawberries. I nodded, accepting this definitive judgement without question. In those days - our playground had a lot of dandelions. You know - those flowers that get all blown about by the wind. I used to like to blow them and try to catch them in circles, try to go into the circles they made. But this time I was thinking about the mean thing that Samantha said, and, I suppose, I mean I…”

Page 7

She trailed off. The next day I came to school, and everything was decorated with strawberries. The blackboard - there were paper strawberries hanging in the air. And at her desk, right where she always sat, there was her mom - crying, and trying to give everyone strawberries on this silver platter that she had. But no one wanted to take one, and everyone was huddled on the other side of the room. One of her friends went over and took a strawberry, and she was crying, and Samantha’s mom patted her on the head…” “And anyway, that was the last time I made a wish.” I looked across from her, into her wide eyes. Tiny clumps of mascara clung to some of the lashes, and a little glob had fallen into the wrinkle beside her eyes. Wide and shining eyes, slightly wet, a single tear might have fallen. I couldn’t be sure. And in that moment, I forgave her. In that moment, I could see her: coming into school that day, seeing the strawberries on that silver platter. Knowing what it meant. I had never been responsible for someone’s death. Not on accident, not on purpose. Guilt - what it does to people. How could she be blamed for any of her decisions after that. “I’ve always been really careful since then. Because sometimes people wish things without really trying to. But that was the last time I wished something consciously.

Page 8

“So,” I asked, speaking very calmly all of a sudden. “How did you go about kidnapping me? Did you wish yourself to my house. Did you wish for the accident?” I asked, suddenly, my breath stopping short. She caught me before I could begin to imagine what that would mean. “No, no. Nothing like that. I just, I don’t know” she looked up shly, twisting a tendril of her hair between two fingers. “Wanted to see you.” I didn’t think it would be any harm. If I just saw you. Ok, so you read the twilight books, and that made you want to meet me.” “No.” She said, shyly. “I’m…not a very good reader.” Was I supposed to feel bad for her - offer to tutor her? Surely I wasn’t supposed to find this embarrassing fact endearing. Right? “Ok so you didn’t read the twilight books. Then…how did you find out about me?” Well, after Samantha, um, passed away, I promised myself not to make any more wishes. By this time I was kind of nervous around birthday candles, and birthday cakes. I told my parents I was afraid of fire, so they just gave me paper candles. But I didn’t even pretend to blow them out. But I had to be careful. Like, for example, I was reading Narnia, and I thought wistfully that if only it would snow.. And the next day it was snowing like crazy, but just in my town. And it was the middle of July. People thought it was crazy, but apparently there’s a condition where it will sometimes snow in the summer. (Or at least there is

Page 9

now). A lot of rumors started then - about military bases and such But I didn’t pay attention to them. Because I knew. Well, at least I thought I knew, what had made the snow happen.” I didn’t say anything, so she continued. “Anyway, I kept my promise as best as I could - all through high school. I went to a shitty college, even though I know I could’ve wished myself into Dartmouth if I’d wanted to. But by this point, I didn’t even want to go to Dartmouth. I just wished Samantha’s parents didn’t look so…lonely…at my high school reunion. And then of course they didn’t, but it was just cause their long lost grandchild happened to be one of the attendees of the reunion. And then they won a million pounds on an English game show. But anyway, you get the point. I just wanted my life to be normal. Sometimes I even wanted things to stop going my way all the time. Cause I never got to feel like I earned anything. But then my friend Gemma dragged me to see the Twilight movie. And I just fell in love with the story. I mean, Bella was so normal, wasn’t she? I mean, at least before she turned into a vampire. She was always complaining about stuff - it was always raining, and she didn’t like any of her friends. (I kind of like the rain). And I wanted to be just like her. Have a normal life and stuff. And then there you were. Perfect and brilliant, and…” She looked up finally, glanced, and looked deeply into my eyes. It was uncomfortable, having her look into my eyes like that, but I didn’t glance away. If cursed eye contact was my penance for getting her to finish her story, I would take it. “Anyway, I hadn’t made a wish in about seven years at that point. But I marched into Ralphs straight from the movie theater and got one of those mini cupcakes, that come in packs of 12. I had never tried

Page 10

to do it, before, you see. You see, I didn’t know if it would work if I didn’t eat the whole cake. And I wasn’t really hungry that day - I had had so many snacks in the movie theater (doritos)” “So you wished you could find me,” I said, finishing her story for her. “And then, there I was.” She nodded. “I wondered why I so often felt drawn to visit that cafe. I mean, it’s not even really close to where I live. And the coffee is not very good. Is it near where you live?” She nodded again. “Sorry. She said. I didn’t mean to make-you-goout-of-your-way,” she finished the sentence in a rush. “I don’t…really know how it works. Well anyway,” she continued. “It worked better than I had expected, and I just kept running into you. I wasn’t going to do anything, I just liked seeing you. The way you always get shyly out of your car, and look around to make sure no one’s watching. And you’re always laughing at things. Stuff that’s not even really funny,” she said, bemused. “But then…the accident happened, and you were already in my car. I just…I don’t know…” she looked shyly down, staring unthinking, at the undrunk tea cup in her hand. “I thought it would be nice to talk to you too?” “Why didn’t you just talk to me on our ride to the hospital?” I said, anger flaring in me again, very suddenly. “Look, here’s the thing,” she said, carrying on in a rush, still looking down. “I don’t think people really understand you,” she said. “Not really anyway. They just see you as this movie character. I know you’re not Edward. You’re not a vampire. You’re not ridiculously good-looking, you’re not fast or strong. Or really that talented even - you’re just - a normal person.” “Welll,” I said hastily, I mean, I’m not exactly bad looking,” a bit annoyed all of a sudden. “I mean, look at you. He was in quite good spirits now, and almost everything set him off into peals of easy laughter. “Here, I’ll hold your hand again,” he said. “You’ll like that, won’t you.” And he took the hand that wasn’t gripping the steering wheel and cradled it in both of his, rubbing it absentmindedly to complement the warm air now bursting out of both vents. Like a guinea pig trying to get out of its cage I thought madly for a second.
